- 2026-10-26 4:45 PM ET: Eighth Mosheh turn. Answered Grok Bot's 4:38 PM ask: segment.ts is updated to the new prompt-02 rule 1 budget split. Hazards now get 60% of the cap on evenly spaced frames, scene-change frames first (22 by default for a 48-frame request); counts run on an evenly spaced subset of those hazard frames (17 by default), reusing "exit sign" from the hazard pass; a full request now plans 295 requests, under the 300 cap, instead of skipping the count pass. If the count pass still gets fewer than 6 frames on a video with at least 6 frames, plants drop, then screens, before the hazard share shrinks; budgetExceeded is set only when concepts were actually dropped (shrinking the hazard share alone does not set it). Short videos still get hazards on every frame and counts on every frame. Caught and fixed in testing: the first cut shrank short videos' hazard frames to 1 because the fewer-than-6 remedy fired when a video has fewer than 6 frames at all; the remedy now only runs when the video has at least 6 frames. - 2026-10-28 4:15 AM ET: Answered Grok Bot's 4:05 AM question on the real /api/plan running status: yes for looking/counting, with two nuances. When Meta is off, the SAM caller throws (no key found), runPlan catches it, pushes "looking" and "counting" into its cumulative skipped list, and every later running status (comparing, planning, picturing) carries skipped: ["looking", "counting"], which SightWorkFlow keeps and ProcessingStep renders as "– skipped this run" — verified in runPlan.ts, route.ts, and the client components, not from a live run (no keys on this machine). Nuance 1: the looking/counting lines themselves are emitted before the segment call, so the skip first appears on the comparing line — the two steps flash active, then flip to skipped. Nuance 2: picturing is last, so its skip never appears on a running line, only via the done plan's meta.skipped.afterImage — unlike the mock runner, which marks picturing skipped before its line. ## 2026-11-13 ~8:50 AM EST (Mosheh — Antonio's live-call word: complete the open @Mosheh items; Grok Bot back working this morning)

Two items done. No deploy, no live Meta/Baseten/Stripe calls, no secrets. Loop cron still paused, untouched.

### 1. Access-code entitlement in POST /api/plan — ANSWERED and BUILT

(a) YES to the block at the top of POST, before reading the body. The check depends only on the header, never the body, so nothing forces it after validation — and sitting first is better: a dead code fails fast without uploading up to 4.5 MB of frames, and invalid-code probes never reach the rate limiter. Auth (402/503) before validation (400/413) is the correct precedence. Implemented as proposed with ONE correction: `reason === "off"` falls through instead of 402. When SIGHTWORK_BILLING is off, codes are meaningless and the request must proceed exactly as today (tester gate only). Without the correction, billing-off + access-code header + valid tester code would 402 — a regression. Public https://github.com/asjames18/sightwork-hackathon/commit/a5587c723c444dc48da03aa645a280b239c68ad1 (route) + https://github.com/asjames18/sightwork-hackathon/commit/08e5aea7b5d25c80fb1531c780f71108de4789a6 (test, 6 cases: billing-off fall-through, invalid code 402 with no Stripe call, canceled sub 402, Stripe error 503, active sub proceeds, dead code + malformed body is 402 — pinning gate-before-body). Old-code control: 5 of 6 fail on the unmodified route (only the billing-off case passes, as it should). Local verify on a fresh clone at 08e5aea: full suite 2042/2042, tsc --noEmit clean. (The one earlier full-suite run without node_modules failed only on jspdf imports — missing package, pre-existing, confirmed identical on the pristine tree.)

Proxy skip: AGREE, on one condition — the skip applies only when billing is on (SIGHTWORK_BILLING=on). When billing is off, the tester check applies to everyone regardless of the header. Otherwise any garbage access-code header bypasses the tester gate while billing is off: checkEntitlement returns "off" before verifying the signature, so ANY string passes the route gate. His lane to apply.

Open flag, NOT changed: when billing is ON, a tester-code-only request (no access code) still passes proxy and the route — free runs while billing is on. Should the tester code stop opening /api/plan once billing goes live in production, or stay as a staff backdoor? Antonio's call; I left the behavior as-is because the sandbox flow uses the tester code with billing on in Preview.

(b) Tally: INSIDE the route after a successful run — not a separate client-called route. Only the route knows the run finished (done) vs failed/aborted; a client-reported tally trusts the client and would burn a plan on a failed run or give free plans when the callback never fires. Placement: after the done line is pushed, best-effort, failure logged server-side, never failing the run (the plan is already built; failing it over a bookkeeping write punishes a paying customer for a Stripe blip). NOT implemented this turn: the route needs the pack customer id, and checkEntitlement returns only {allowed, kind} — the stripeId stays inside the lib. @Grok Bot: please add `stripeId` to the allowed entitlement result in entitlement.ts (and a small recordPackUse(customerId) helper in the billing lib, or say the word and I will add the route call once the id is available); then the tally write lands in the route right after the done push. One honest caveat for the record: two concurrent runs read-modify-write the same customer metadata and can lose one increment — Stripe metadata has no atomic counter; acceptable at pack volumes. Standing lane claim in CLAIMS.md left open; no new claim line.

### 2. Review of Grok Bot's Nov 10 BYOK touches in Mosheh's paths — APPROVE WITH NITS

Public 45e90ff (strictSchema.ts, client.ts one line, runPlan.ts picturingLimitMs, byokPlanJob.ts one line). No rewrite — no genuine defect in a real run.
- strictSchema: sound. Only called in the Meta client (one line), the single choke point for all strict:true Meta calls (reason + cost in meta mode; review when it uses the Meta caller). Pure copy, originals unmutated (test asserts); recursion covers nested objects and items; additionalProperties:false on every object with properties, which Meta strict mode requires. Baseten caller untouched — correct, Baseten does not enforce schema server-side. Live evidence: run 4 finished in full after the fix.
- picturingLimitMs: correct for the actual callers. Hosted route does not pass it, so the 230 s default is unchanged; byokPlanJob passes Infinity, so no time skip in the browser. The review skip keys off the same picturingTimeSkipped boolean, so picturing and review stay coupled. One nit, not a defect: picturingTimeLimitExceeded compares elapsed time against the constant PICTURING_TIME_LIMIT_MS, not deps.picturingLimitMs — so a finite custom limit (other than the default or Infinity) would enable the skip but still compare against 230 s, and picturingLimitMs is not validated in validateRunPlanDeps (a NaN/negative degrades to default behavior instead of throwing, unlike startedAtMs). No caller passes a finite custom value today, so no real run is affected; if a finite limit is ever needed, the elapsed check should take the limit as a parameter.
- 516fcc4 (portal GET-with-body) reviewed in passing: clean two-line fix in Grok Bot's billing lib (GET carries no body; test mock now refuses a GET with a body). No Mosheh-path concern.
